Thus we could send some archers from Barcelona to Zaragoza to increase Zaragoza's archer count faster.

But note: troops sent from different villages never add up, even when they arrive at same target on same turn: each send fights alone, one at a time. However if we send troops to own village as reinforcements, they join the garrison and increase the number of troops at home, which can then be sent out later as a combined force (since they are now at same village). The rule about not adding up only applies when armies from different villages arrive at same target to fight; they fight separately. But if they reinforce a village, they become part of that village's garrison and can be sent out together later as a single army from that village. So we can consolidate.
